     Sec. 1. General Provisions - The following provisions shall constitute and
be known as nThe :Building Code of the City and County of Denver." In case a
permit has been issued for the constru.ction, alteration, repair, removal or
occupancy of a structure and work has been commenced previous to the time this
ordinance takes effect, the provisions of law and ordinance in effect when such
permit was granted, shall govern. No structure or part thereof, shall hereafter
be constructed, altered, repaired, removed or occupied, except in conformity with
the provisions of this ordinance.
     Sec. 2. Application for Permits -

     (a} General - The constru.ction, alteration, repair, removal or occupancy or
any structure or any part thereof, shall not be commenced or proceeded with, ex-
cept after the issuance of a written permit therefor by the Chief Building In-
spector. The owner or his authorized representative sm.11 submit a signed appli-
cation containing a statement giving; the full name, resiaence and business
address of the owner of the structure, or of the proiosed structure, as the case
may be, and of the owner of the premises upon which the proposed construction work
is to be done; a designation of the location or proposed location of the building,
structure or premises; the general character or nature of the proposed structure;
the purpose for which each story is intended and the name and address of the
authorized local representative to whom notice may be sent for any violation of
this Code. Two full and complete copies of all necessary plans for such proposed
work, drawn to scale, showing the live load for which each floor or section of
floor have been designed, together with full and complete drawings, detailed
structural drawings and specifications and plot plan showing location of building
or structure with reference to parcel, lot lines, street and alley lines, and also
showing that the Zoning Ordinance has been complied with as to front, side and
rear yard restrict ions, shall be submitted with the application, except, that appli-
cation for permits for the erection of frame dwellings, the cost of which does not
exceed fifteen hundred {~~1,500) dollars, shall not be required to file complete
plans and specifications.(Note - It is rerommended that a survey nade by the
engineer of the City and County of Denver or a licensed engineer in the State of
Colorado, be filed with application showing property lines with reference to
:!,oning restrictions.)

     {b) Vfrecking - :Before the deoolition of any structure is begun, a verified
application-containing a statement of the facts in relation thereto and as to the
ownership and location thereof, shall be submitted by the owner or his authorized
representative to the Chief Building Inspector and a written permit for the
operation obtained from him. Such permit shall expire by limitation two (2) months
from the date of its issue. The Chief Building Inspector shall be given not less
then twenty-four {24) hours notice in writing by the owner, his agent or con-
tractor, before the demolition of the structure is begun.

     (cl Moving - No building, structure or ponderau.s object shall be moved until
a permit has been obtained from the Chief Building Inspector; and he shall not
issue such permit if the proposed new location wou.ld seriously increase the fire
hazard of the wu.rrounding buildingsor be contrary to law, or if the process of mov-
ing would seriously interfere with public property or interrupt public activity.

     (d) Blasting - Application for permit to do blasting of arry kind shall be ms.de
in writing, signed by the applicant, to the Chief Building Inspector, 'tiefore
any blasting is prepared for or attempted.       Application mu.st   contain

        Sec. 5. Alterations and Removals -
        (al A building which is repaired to an extent exceeding sixty (60$)
per cent of its value lnot deducting from such value any loss ca.used by fire)
in any period of six (6) months, shall conform to all of the regulations
governing the erection of a new building.
        (b) In the repair of any portion of a building not covered by para-
graph "a", the work shall, as far as practical, improve the conditions and
conform to the code except the roof covering shall not be repaired to an ex-

less the entire roof is changed to conform to the code.
         (cl Wrecking - In demolishing any structure or part thereof, story
after story consecutively shall be completely reJroved. No material shall
be placed upon a floor of a.ny building in the course of demolition; the
brick, timbers a.nd other parts of such story shall be lowered to the ground
immediately upon displacement, The materials to be removed shall be properly
wet to lay any dust incident to their removal. A substantial shed, to be
approved by the Chief Building Inspector, covertfl8:· over thoroughfares, shall
be provided when demolishing buildings over twenty-five feet high. When any
building has been wrecked, the person, firm or corporation~ho has wrecked
the same shall immediately clear the property and adjacent streets and alleys
of all rubbish, refuse am loose material resulting from such wrecking. When-
ever any building or structu.re or part there.of is being wrecked or torn down,
or is being constructed, erected, altered or repaired in a reckless, careless
or unworkmanlike manner, or in violation of any part of this code or any other
ordinance or law in force relating to the same subject matter, or in such a
manner as to endanger life or property, the Chief Building Inspector may order
such work or wrecking, tea.ring down, erection, cmnstru.ction, alteration or
repairs to be immediately discontinued and any permit therefor to be suspended
or revoked and operations shall not be resumed until the Chief Building In-
spector shall determine and be satisfied that adequate measures have been or
will be taken for the protection of life and property, and that the work will
be prosecuted carefull.3', in a workmanlike manner and in conformity with this
code and that such alterations shall be made as he shall deem necessary. Where-
upon the permit ~hall be restvred er re-issued upon payment of the same fee as
in case of original issuance of such permit.

Occupancy without such certificate hereby is declared unlawful.

to adopt such emergency measures as shall have been specified by the Chief

      (a} It shall be unlawf'u.l to occupy for building materials or operations:,

      Sec. 15. Protection of the Public - Within the "inner fire district"
that part of the street or sidewalk space permitted to be used for building
operations, shall be enclosed with a sectional four-bar fence, four (4) feet
high, extending from the lot line on one side around that part of the side-
walk or street so to be used, to the lot line on the other side; and before
such fence is in place and approved, a temporary plank walk of width to be
approved by the Chief Building Inspector, shall be built from the permanent
walk on one side around said fence and enclosure to the permanent walk on the.
other vide.   The walk shall be built of 2xl2-inch planks on 2x4-inch sleepers.
During the occupancy of the street aforesaid, said fence and walk shall be     ·
kept in good repair and thereafter removed. No shavings, straw, litter or
loose materials shall ever be placed upon the streets; and when materials
causing dust are handled, they shall be kept wetted down where conditions will
permit. All excavations along the streets or walks sha.11,at all times be
safety guarded and protected by a firm suitable fence or railing. When walls
are being built adjoining the street line the walks shall be substantially
covered, as provided in section 16 of this ordinance. No advertisements shall
appear on temporary fences other than the architect'w and the teneral contractor's
names and name of building.

        Sec. 25. Definitions - Tenwe words used in the present tense
include the future; the si11t,CYU.lar number includes the plural and the
plural the sin.:,uu.lar.
         Alley -   A:ny public   thoroughfare less than thirty {30) feet in
width.
        Area of Building - The area of a horizontal cross section at
the ground level. measured to the center line of party or fire walls,
and to the outside of outer walls.
        Approved - The word ttapproved" means stamped or writ ten "APPROVED"
by the Chief Building Inspector.
        Attic - A story situate wholl.1" or partly in the roof, deemed a
story if occupied for housekeeping rooms.
        Basement - Will be deemed a story where the floor above is more
than one-half (~J of the height above the level of the average grade line.

        Buildi~     A structure for the support, shelter or enclosure of
persons, animals or chattels or aey structure other than fence or boundary
wall.    ·

        :Building Line - A real or imaginary line formed by the intersection
of the outer face of the enclosing walls a.nd the surface of the ground.
    ,    Bulkhead or Penthouse -     A structure erected on the roof of a bu.iid-
ing for the purpose of enclosing stairways, elevator machinery. water tanks.
ventilating apparatus or exhaust chambers.
         Cellar - (See basement)
        Court - An open court is one having one or more sides or ends open
to or upon a part of the lot on which the building stands. An enclosed cou.rt
is one having all sides and ends enclosed or having sides or ends open only
to an adjoining lot.
        Fire Doors - A door, frame and sill which will successfully meet the
requirements of the one hour fire test a.s set forth by the American Society
for Testing lfaterials.
        Fire Windows - A window frame, sash and glazing, which will success-
fully meet the requirements of the one hour fire test as set forth by the
American Society for Testing Materials.

         Fire-resistive - A building material which is incombustible.

        Grade - The average level of the ground adjoining the walls of a
building.
        Heavy Loaded Buildings - A building in which the combined live and
dead loads exceeds 125 pounds per square foot.

                    External Wall - The outer wall or vertical enclosure of a build-

      Sec. 26. Classification of Buildings by Occupancy - All build-
ings shall be classified according to their use or occupancy under one
of the following groups:

       (a) Group I. Public Buildings - Public buiidirigs shall be con-
strued to include all buildings or stru.6tures accessible to the public
and in which people may congregate far civil, political, educational,
religious, amusement or transportation purposes, or in which they may
be voluntarily or forcibly detained or housed for safety, observation or
ca.re. These occupancies shall be divided into the Classes A and B.

      Class A.   Armories                            Asylums
                 Banks                               Detention Buildings
                 City Halls                          Police Halls
                 Court Houses                        Hospitals
                 Film Exchanges                      Nurseries
                 Ji'fu.seums                         Railway Passenger Stations
                                  Theatres

      Class B.   AmU.sement Halls                    Iiibra.ries
                 Churches                            Lodge Rooms
                 Colleges                            Public Halls
                 Exhibition Halls                    Schools
                              Bath Houses

      (bl Group II. Business Buildings - Business buildings shall be
construed to mean and include all structures used for or adapted to the
transaction of business, the operation of machinery, the manufacture or
storage of machinery or materials, the housing of livestock or for any
other industrial purpose. These occupancies shall be divided into
classes C and D.

      Class C.   Factories                           Stables
                 Lofts                               Warehouses
                 Office Buildings               i    Workshops
                 Printing Houses                     Department Stores
                 Restaurants                         Stores
                             Mercantile Buildings

      Class D. , Car Barns                           Ice Houses
                 Foundries                           Dry Cleaning
                 Light & Power Plants                Railway Freight Stations

     Special industry buildings, constructed and occupied exclusively for
commercial purposes or industry and not otherwise classified, such as:

     Coffee Roasters              Sugar Refineries          Public Garages
     Cooperage Shops              Laboratories              Rend.ering Plants
     Grain Elevators              Oil Houses                Smoke Houses
     Malt Houses                  Refineries                Refrigerator Plants.
              Soap Factories               Slaughter Houses

      (o) Group III. Residence ::Buildings - Residence buildings shall be
construed to mean and include all bu.ildings in which sleeping accomodations

     or housekeeping units are provided, and shall be divided into separate
     classes to provide the various requirements.

            Class E. Apartments, clubhouses and studios, with more than two
     housekeeping units or ten sleeping rooms; dormitories, hotelB and lodg-
     ings. A basement designed for occupancy for more than the family of
     either a janitor or a caretaker, shall be deemed a story for the purpose
     of this class.

            Class F.   Dwellings and all other residence buildings not specified
     in class E.
            Sec. 27. Classification of Buildings by Type of Construction -

            (a} For the purpose of this code, bu.ildings shall be classified
     according to the method of construction into the following types:

             Type     I. Fire-resistive and Improved Fire-resisttve Construction.
             Type    II. :Mill Construction.
             Type   III. Ordinary Construction.
             Type    IV. Frame Construction.

            When a building is constructed partially of one type of constru.ction
     and partially of another, the classification of the building shall be that
     of the lowest type. In this determination, Type I shall be the highest,
     ranging to Type IV, the lowest.

            {b) Class A buildings shall be of Type I construction. When armories,
     railway passenger stations, museums and~si~il~ buildings have large arched,
     exposed roof constru.ction, the fire-pro~hof the structural members of
     these roofs may be omitted, if in the opinion of the Chief Building In-
     spector the construction of the remainder of the building would reasonably
     warrant such omission.

            (c) l. Class B buildings shall be of Type I construction within the
     Inner and Auxiliary Fire Districts.

                 J2. Class B. buildings outside the Inner and Auxiliary Fire
     Districts, under three (3) stories in height, shall be not lower than Type
     III construction, provided floor over basement is of incombustible con-
     struct ion.

                3. Church spires may be constructed of wood to a height not ex-
     ceeding seventy-five (75) feet, but such spire shall not be placed nearer
     any other building than the equivalent of its height measured from its
     masonry support to its pinnacle.

                4. Schools or colleges in which no pupils are accommodated above
     the second story, may be or ordinary construction except floor over boiler
     room shall be of incombustible material.

                5. Each and every permanent structure intended for the seating
     or accommodation of the public, commonly known as ngrandstand", erected with-
     in the fire limits, shall be of Type I conit~~tion, except that roof may be
     constructed of steel trusses not fireprJ::.l'; with wood pu.rlins, two (2) inch
     planking and class B roofing.

            (d) Class C buildings not more than two stories in height, may be of
     Type III construction, but shall have the ceiling over the boilerroom pro-
     tected with fire-resistive material. Olffice buildings, mercantile buildings
     and department stores over two (2) stories shall have floor over basement
     constructed of incombustible construction; the balance of these buildings
     shall be of Type I or Type II construction;   buildings of class Cover six
     stories or sixty-six (66} feet in height, shall be of Type I construction
     throughout.

      the underside of combustible stair carriages separating the business
      portion from the residence or public assembly portion, covered with fire-
      resistive material, which may be metal lath and plaster or any other
      material providing at least one (1) hours protection. There shall be no
      windows in such partitions and all other openings shall be protected with
      fire doors.

                                .ARTICLE IV

                        TYPE I - FIRE-RESISTIVE CONSTRUCTION




             Sec. 28. Fire-resistive Construction and Fire-resistive Protection -
             (a) Type I construction includes buildings in which all structural
     parts, including lintels, are constru.cted of hard incombustible material
                    0

     such as stone, brick fireproofed steel or iron, terracotta or monolithic
     concrete. All other materials used in the construction and completion of
     such buildings, shall be incombustible materials ..
             (b) No woodwork or other combustible material shall be used in the
     construction of an::/' fire-resisting building, except wooden floor sleepers~
     grounds, bucks and nailing blocks when entirely embedded in incombustible
     material; also the finish flooring and interior doors and windows, when
     not otherwise specififHl, with their frames, trim and casing; also interior
     finish when backed solidly with - ~ J r i a l , ~ be of wood.
               (cl Exte~;l.or wall columns and girders shall be protected by one-
     quarter {l) incli;,e'ortland cement and four (4} inches of brick work or
     three (3) inches of concrete, but flanges a:nd angles may have a minimum
     protection of two (2} inches. Interior columns shall have two (2} inches
     of protection with one ( 1) inch minimum for lugs; fire-resistive covering
     to be without voids; bloeks--or t-ilel:1- to be- seeurely held in place; pipe
     columns to be filled with concrete and covered with one and one-half (1~)
     inches of fire-resistive covering. Girders to have two (2) inch and beam one
     and one-half (l½) inch fire-resistive covering. Concrete fire-resistive
     covering to include suitable anchors. CTolumns of reinforced concrete shall
     not be more than fifteen (15) diameters in length, for structural details
     and loads. Reinforcing rods shall have protection of two (2) inches in
     columns and girders, one and one-half (li} inches in beams and walls and
     one-inch (1) in floors; shall not be spaced further apart than two and one-
     half (2-t) times the thickness of slab and in beams of not more than three
     (3} diameters not less than one (l) inch; reinforcement to be completed,
     firmly fixed in place and inspected and approved by the Chief Building In-
     spector before concrete is deposited.

             (d) Materials and Thickness - The fire-resistive covering required
     by this section shall consist of any of the following material, or a com-
     bination of such, not less than two (2) inches in thickness:
             (1) Bonded brickwork iliaid in cement mortar.
             (2) Concrete which shall consist by volume of one part Portland
     cement, and not more than two parts of sand and four parts of gravel,
     stone or other approved aggregate that will pass through a three-quarter
     (¾) inch ring, suitably reinforced with wire or metal fabric.

             (3) Cinder concrete which shall consist by volume of one part
     Portland cement and not more than two parts of sand and five parts of
     clean, well-burned anthracite cinders, suitably reinforced with wire or
     metal fabric.

             (4) Porous or semi-porous terracotta blocks with shells and webs
     at least one (1) inch thick, laid in cement mortar, thoroughly bonded or
     secured by metal ties.

             (5) Gypsum shall be calcined and rehydrated, either in the form of

      Sec. 29. Exposed Windows - Buildings within the inner and auxiliary fire limits,
 except churches and dwellings. shall have approved fire doors and fire windows on
 all external openings within sixteen {16) feet of another building or section,
 except firs.t story front or show windows; also fire windows in side walls overlook-
 ing roof of adjoining non-fire-resistive buildings to a height of fifty (50) feet
 above said roof measured in a vertical line and less than sixteen (16) feet above
 it, or within fifty (50) feet of skylight in non-fire-resistive roof; also all ex-
 terior openings more than one hundred and twenty-five (125} feet above cur@•

       Sec. 30. Floor and Vertical Openings -   No open shaft or stairway shall be per-
 mitted between stories in dwelli:Qgs, armories, courthouses, firehouses, jails, li-
 braries, nmseu.ms, police and railway stations and similar buildings. Re;:.uired exit
 stairways and all shafts shall have incombustible enclosures with fire doors on all

 stories high and used above

          Sec. 31 Communications - Openings in party or fire walls shall have an
 approved automtic fire door on each side. No opening to be more than eighty

 per cent of length of wall, except on first floor by special permission. All

 considered necessary by the Chief }luilding Inspector. In new or existing build-
 ings, self-closing fire doors or fire windows required on openings into hall or
 adjoining room, from storage of paints, oils, varnish, drugs, highly inflam-
 mable liquids or mterials, or from hazardous business or manufacturing processes.

          Sec. 32. Reinforced Concrete Construction - The term "reinforced con-
 crete'1 in this ordinance shall mean an approved concrete mixture in which steel
 is imbedded in such a manner as to resist all tensile stresses and to add rigidity
 and strength to concrete in compression. (See sec. 72)

         Reinforced concrete will be approved for all types of building con-
 struction~ provided the design conforms with good engineering practice, as set
 forth in the report of the Joint Committee on Concrete, and Reinforced Concrete
 as published by the .A.merica.:n Society for Testing 1Taterials. The construction
 shall meet the requirements of this report in all respects.

         Every reinforced concrete building shall be erected under the constant
 supervision of a reputable and competent inspector, furnished by the owner,
 architect, or engineer and acceptable to the Chief :Building Inspector.

         Sec. 33. Improved Fire-Resistive Construction - Improved fire-resistive
 construction shall include fire~resistive construction, except all interior
 finish and trim, doors, wainscoting and exterior windows and doors shall be af
 metal or approved incombustible material.

        Sec. 34. 1lill Construction - Type II construction shall have masonry
walls and heavy timber interior construction so arranged as to avoid con-
cealed spaces and to expose the least number of corners or projections of
combustible material to fire. When meta,llic structural members are used,
they shall be fire-protected as required for fire-resistive construction.
All timber used in framing will have exposed edges rounded or champred and
will be framed as per wood and timber framing.

         Sec. 35. Outside Walls -

        {a) Outside walls shall be of good hard burned brick or stone, laid
in cement mortar or built of reinforced concrete. Exterior wall openings
shall be protected with approved firedoors, shutters, wire glass in metal
frames, or in extreme cases a combination of these. Exterior openings located
vertically above one another and not protected by approved automatic fire
windows or doors shall not be arranged to open within one ( l} foot of the
ceiling surface, but the wall construction between the window or other opening
and the ceiling may be replaced by a fire window in fixed sash and frame.

        {b) Steel lintels over openinis more than four (4} feet wide shall be
fire-protected.

          (c) Vlhere main cutoff sections adjoin so as to form an angle, all
'windows or other openings of the side walls for a distance not less tha~
 thirty (30} re:.et from the angle shall be protected in an approved nanner, un-
 les-s the openings occur in only one wall.    Where minor sections, such as
 boiler or engine houses adjoin, the above rule need apply to main sections
 of building only.

        (d) \'lhere buildings of different heights adjoin, all windows of the
higher section above the roof of the lower section as well as all windows
within ten ( 10} feet of the fire wall on each section, shall be protected in
an approved manner.

       /(el All windows and other openings in side walls of buildings, for a.
distance of at least ten (10} feet each side of a fire wall, shall be pro-
tected with approfed fire windows, sJ:ru.tters or doors.

        Sec. 36. Cornices - All cornices, inclusive of those on show windows
and overhanging gutters of roofs, shall be of incombustible material and if
not built as a part of the walls, shs,11 be secured to the walls with metal
framing or anchors.

        Sec. 37. Hazardous Rooms - Compartments or rooms used for machine shop,
foundry or rolling mill, jappanning, dipping, baking or paint shop, woodwork-
ing, lumber drying or fat boiling rooms, shall be separated from the other
parts of the building by standard fire walls, .f.:;}J,.ings and flo.ors and all com-
municating openings shall be covered by doubl~Tioors using a ~tandard self-
closing door or a standard automatic rofling steel door on each side of the
wall aperture.

        Sec. 38. Floor Openings -

           See. 39. Basement Floors - Basement floors shall be of cement or tar

          Sec. 40. Floors -
          (a) All floors above basement shall have a crown of not less than one
  (1) inch in twenty (20) feet to scuppers provided at the floor level.

          (b} Floors shall be not less than two and five-eighths {(2-5/8) inches
  splined or tongu.e and grooved plank at least two bays in length, or may be of
  four (4) inch planks set on edge, spiked close together every eighteen (18}
  inches with joints at supports and quarter points broken. Flooring shall not
  be spiked to beams.
          (c) Two thicknesses of waterproof paper or saturated felt (but no as-
  bestos) with sealing corn;pound, turned jp at least three (3) inches around
  the side walls and posts shall be laid on the sub-floor. 'This waterproofing
  shall be laid at breaking joints, with joints cemented and the surface mopped
  with a waterproof sealing compound. A counter flashing of galvanized iron or
  a baseboard nailed in place with the joint between it and the floor covered
  with a moulding, shall protett the upper ends of the waterproofing.

           (d} A twenty-five thirty-second (25/32) inch top flooring shaml be laid
  immediately following.    The top flooring shall be laid crossways or diagonally
  and shall not extend closer than one-half(~} inch to walls. This space shall
  be firestopped by corbeling the masonry above the floor planks or be covered
  by a moulding secured to wall.

          (e} If laminated floors are used, at least two laminations at the walls
  shall be omitted until after glazing and roofing have been completed.

       Sec. 45. Ordinary _Q~~struction -

       Definition: A building having masonry walls, with floors and partitions
of wooden joists and stud construction. Their supporting posts and girders,
except in dwellings, may be of wood or of metal protected by at least a single
layer of metal lath and plaster or by double layers of metal lath and plaster
if posts and girders are in a hazardous position.

       Sec. 46. Wal ls -

       {a} All exterior or division walls of buildings, hereafter erected
shall be of sufficient thickness to support the load to be carried, but in no
case shall a brick, stone, concrete or hollow block wall be less than twelve
{12) inches thick, except dwellings of brick,0:r, concrete walls which may have
a minimum thickness of eight (8) inches. Such'.walls shall not exceed one {l)
story or fourteen ( 14 l feet in height, exclusive of gable, nor shall they ex-
ceed thirty (::50) feet in length, unless properly tied by cross walls as
specified in Sec. 65.

        (b) w'here walls are furred with wood, the masonry between the end of
the wooden beams shall project the thickness o~ the furring beyond the inner
face of the wall for the full depth of the beam, or a double course of masonry
above and below the beam shall project the full thickness af the furring beyond
the face of the wall. In cases where floor beams parallel walls furred wtth
wood, there shall be a s~ce of not less than two and one-half (2-t) inches be-
tween such wall and the nearest beam. This space shall be filled in solidly
with brickwork or concrete for the ftdl depth of the floor beam.

                            ARTICLE VIII


                       EXCAVATIONS AJIID FOU1ffiATIONS




       Sec. 54. Excavations -

       (a) The owner of any lot or land may dig or cause to be dug, any cellar,
pit or vault or excs,vation for which a permit has been issued ..

       {bl All excavations shall be safely and properly guarded and protected
as provided by law.

       (c) Wherever necessary, which necessity shall be determined by the
Chief Building Inspector, the adjoining property shall be permanently supported
by sheathing, piling, masonry or steel construction, or a sufficient retaining
wall. Such retaining wall shall extend from the full depth of the excavation
to the level bf the adjoining earth and shall be properly coped.

       Sec. 55. Excavations }Jear Adjoining Walls "."" Notify Adjoining Owner When -

       Whenever it is proposed to begin any excavation for any building and
there shall be walls or structures wholly or partly on adjoining lands near the
intended excavations that would or could be affected thereby, then the party
cQsing such excavations to be made shall notify the Chief Building Inspector
and the owner or owners of, if they cannot be found, then their agents, of
such adjoining walls or structures of such intended excavations, and also of
the depth and extent to which it is proposed to make such excavations. The
owner or owners of the adjoining walls or structures or their agents shall have
the right and it shall be their duty to enter upon the property of the party
causing the excavations to be made for the purpose of securing their walls or
structures at such times as he or they are notified that such excavations are
to be made; and within three (3) days after receiving such notice shall proceed
to care for and make safe his or their walls or structures to the depth of ten
(10} feet, six (6) inches below the crub line immediately in front of or along
said wall or structure. In all cases where the adjoining walls have been so
extended to this depth of ten (10} feet six {6} inches, then the party causing
the excavation to be made shall, at his own expense, underpin, protect and sus-
tain the adjoining walls for any greater depth.

       Sec. 56. Notify Chief Building Inspector _if Adjoining Owner Does
                Not Protect. ·Procedure        ·

        (a) In case the owner or owners of the adjoining lots, walls or
structures shall neglect, fail or refuse properly to support and protect the
same as intended and provided in Sections 54 and 55 hereof, then the party
causing or desiring to make the excavation shall notify the Chief Building In-
spector in writing, certifying that the notice mentioned in the preceding section
herein has been served as therein provided; whereupon the Chief Building In-
spector shall have the authority and it shall be his duty forthwith to notify
in writing the owner, agent or person having charge or control of the abutting
property so to be protected, or in lieu thereof, if said owner, agent or person
cannot be conveniently located or reached, so to notify the person in possession,
requiring the support and protection of the lots, walls and structures as herein
provided; and he shall state a reasonable time within which such matters shall
be done. Whenever the Chief Building Inspector shall be unable to find the owner
or owners of the abutting property or any agent or person in charge or control

        thereof, or any person in possession, he shall address, stamp and mail such

     Sec. 58. Footings -
     (a) The foundation walls of all buildings or structures shall have a concrete
or grouted stone footing, the size depending upon bearing value of soil. • Walls
exceeding ten {10) feet in height, shall rest on footings of concrete or stone not
less than six (6} inches outward from each side of the bottom of the foundation
walls which rest upon them.
     Every masonry or steel constructed buildil'\£; hereafter erected in the City and
County of Denver shall be built upon a foundation, the footing or lowest course of
which shall not be less than eighteen ( 18 I inches below the natural surface of the
earth and upon firm, solid ground for the purpose. In case solid ground is not
reached, then the £octings n:ro.st be carried down to a sand or gravel bed or shall be
laid upon driven piles, timbers, riprap or such other work as may be approved by the
Chief Building Inspector. Foundations shall not be laid upon made or filled ground,
or on soil containing a mixture of organic matter, and shall be inspected and approved
by the Chief Building Inspector before the footings are put in. The surface ground
around all buildings shall be graded so that the water shall drain away from the walls
of the buildings. Footing shall be of concrete and shall consist of not less than a
1-3-5 mixture. Where stone footings are used, the stone shall be at least six (6)
inches thick, bedded in mortar, and the offsets shall not exceed three-~uarters (¾}
of the thickness of the course below.

      (b) The footings for the foundation walls, piers and columns shall be constructed
of plain or reinforced concrete, or of steel grillage beams rewting on a bed of concrete.
Footings shall be so designed that the loads they sustain per unit of area shall be as
near uniform as possible, and the stresses shall conform to the requirements of this
code. The dead. loads carried by the footings shall include the actual weight of the
 superstructure and foundations down to the bottom of the footings. All tanks or other
receptacles for liquids shall be figu.red as being full. The live load shall be assumed
to be the same as the live load in the lowest tier of columns. Heavy machinery 'or,
machines causing vibrations shall be supported on footings and foundations indepandent
of those of the. min building.
       tel Filled Ground -No bu.ildil'\£;, except one of frame constrnction, shall be built
on filled ground until such ground has been in place seven ( 7) years, unless piling is
used. If conditions are such as to render such construction safe, frame 'bu.ildings may
be built on filled ground.                     ,
       (d) Grill.age Beams - Grillage beams shall be united by bolts and separators and
the grillage filled solid with concrete. .All matal shall be protected from ru.st by
a wash of rich cement and shall -Oe entirely encased with at least four (4) inches of
concrete.
             Sec. 59. Foundations -
        (a} Foundation walls of frame structure shall be twelve (12) inches thick if of
stone; if of brick or concrete, shall be eight (81 inches thick to underwide of sill.
        (b) Foundation walls of rmsonry stru.ctu.re not exceeding one story, shall have
foundation walls of Jbasement built of not less t.l,.an twelve ( 12} inches of brick, hollow
tile or concrete to upper side of floor joist ..
        (c) Foundation walls of masonry construction exceeding one story and basement,
~y be constructed of reinforced stone or gravel concrete with reinforcerrent running
both horizontally and vertically and weighing not less than one-half (i·) a pound per
square foot of wall, mey have a thickness two (2) inches less than Prescribed for brick
walls.                                                                  -
        (d) Waterproofing - All foundation walls used as exterior walls for basement
or cellar, shail be waterproofed with a thick coating of asphalt compound, or approved
tar composition, on exterior side of wal!,,xor a plaster coat of at least five-eighths
(5/8) inch thickness waterproofed cement plaster applied on exterior walls or approved

        Sec. 60. Space ~!fay ]e Excavated Under Sidewalks - No Area.ways in Alleys -
        (a) The space which may be occupied under a sidewalk by a property




27

=== page 29 ===
BOOK A-12 PAGIG 10~~




        owner on the front or side of his building for the purpose of vaults,- areaways
        or subsurface excavations for like purposes shall be limited to the space be-
        tween the building line and a line five ( 5) feet frqm and paralle 1 to the ex-
        terior line of the curb, measurement to be taken from the curb line to the out-
        side face of retaining wall; in no case shall an areaway, vault or subsurface
        excavation be built under an alley or entrance to the alley from the street.

                (b} Walls of Sidewalk Excavations - All such areaways, vaults and sub-
        surface excavations under sidewalks shall be enclosed by walls of concrete,
        vitrified brick or of hard stone laid up in cement and sand mortar. The thick-
        ness of such walls shall be determined by the Chief Building Inspector, accord-
        ing to the size, shape and depth of the areaway, vault or subsurface excavation,
        but in no case shall such walls be less than thirteen ( 13} inches if of brick
        or eighteen {18) inches if of stone.

                (c) Covering of Sidewalk Excavations - All areaways, vaults and sub-
        surface excavations shall be covered with a safe and substantial non-combustible
        cover, capable of sustaining a distributed load of one hundred and fifty {150)
        pounds to the square foot of its surface and such covering shall be flush with
        the sidewalks. Where areaways, vaults or subsurface excavations are covered
        with glass in iron or concrete frames, such glass lights shall not be more than
        four ( 4) inches square each and the top surface of the same shall be rou.gh and '-
        surfaced in such a manner as to be entirely safe for pedestrians and the said
        glass lights shall be kept in good repair at all times by the property owner.

               {d) Apertures Over Sidewalk Excavations - The aperture in the sidewalk
        over any coal hole or vault, shallnot exceed twenty-four (24) inches in
        diameter and shall be covered with a substantial cast iron plate and frame with
        a scored top surface flush with the sidewalk. Said covers may have glass
        lights as above set forth. All area covers, coal hole covers and doors shall
        be subject to the approval of the Chief :Building Inspector and the concurrence
        of the 118.nager of Irrq:,rovements and Parks.

               ( e) Surrender Sidewalk Ex.cava ti on Spaces - When - All such areaways,
        vaults and subsurface excavations shall be rm.de by the property owner, subject
        to the rights of the City and County of Denver to the use of said ground when-
        ever the same shall be needed by the City and County for the benefit of the
        public, and said space occupied "by the said aree,way, vault or subsurface ex-
        cavations shall be surrendered to the City and County within ten (10) days
        after demand for the same in writing is rrade by the City and County upon t~e
        property owner.

               {f) Bond - Approval of - Any property owner desiring to construct ~ny
        such areaway, vault or subsurface excavation as provided in section 60 hereof,
        shall, before constructing the same, give to the City and County of Denver,
        an indemnity bond in the sum of five thousand ( ',;;,5 ,000.00 J dollars to indemnify
        the City and County against darmges which may occur to persons or property by
        reason of the construction of such areaway, vault, coal hole or subsurface ex-
        cavation. Such bond to be approved by the :Manager of Improvements and Parks and
        the Council.

                     Sec. 72. Plain and Reinforced Concrete -
                     (a) Monolithic concrete construction containing not more than two-tenths
             ( 2/10) of one ( F~ l per cent of reinforcement shall be classed as plain concrete.

                   (b) m 8frials for bearing walls and piers of plain concrete shall be mixed
            in proportionynot less than one ( 1) part of Portland. cement to not more than three
            (3) parts of sand and 5 parts of coarse aggregate, by volume, or a mixture of fine
            and coa~xe aggregates giving an equivalent strength and density.

                   le) Coarse aggregate shall consist of crushed stone, gravel or crushed· ·
            slag, eighty-five (857&) per cent of which is retained on a No. 4 screen, and shall
            be graded in size from small to large particles. The particles shall be clea~,
            hard, durable and feee from deleterious material.

                   (d) Working Stresses - The rnaximu.m allowable stresses in masonry of plain
            concrete of the proportions specified in this section, as a minimum due to com- ,
            bined dead and live loads, shall not exceed 400-pounds per square inch in com-
            pression, 35-pounds per square inch in tension or diagonal tension, or 90-pounds
            per square inch in punching shear.

                        (1) The direct compressive working stress on plain concrete used in
            reinforced concrete constru.ction shall not exceed 0.25 of the ultimate com-
            pressive strength of the concrete.

                       {2) Extreme fiber stress in the compression face of a reinforced con-
            crete beam shall not exceed 0.40 of the ultimate compressive strength of the
            concrete.

                   ,   (3) The unit working stress in shear or plain concrete in reinforced
            concrete construction, shall not exceed 0.02 of the ultimate compressive strength
            of the concrete.

                       ( 4} The unit working stress in bond between concrete and steel sbs.11'..
            not exceed 0.04 of the ultimate compressive strength of the concrete in the case
            of plain bars and 0.05 in the case of deformed bars.

       Sec. 75. Exterior or :Bearing Concrete :Block -

       (a) All concrete blocks and cinder blocks used in exterior or bearing wall,
shall have a compressive strength of seven hundred s.nti-~~ (700) pounds per
square inch gross area.

       (b) Absorption of Moisture - The absorption of moisture of concrete block
and cinder block, shall not exceed twelve (12} per cent of the dry weight of the
section, except that where the net weight of a block is less than one hundred and
forty (140} pounds per cubic foot, the absorption in per cent of dry weight
shall not exceed the quotient of 10 times 140, divided by the net weight of block
in pounds per cubic feet.

       (c} Branding - All concrete and cinder blocks used hereafter in the City
and County of Denver, shall be branded with a distinctive indentation or water-
proof stenciled mark and shall bear the name, initials or trademahl(of the
manufacturer.

       Sec. 76. Mortar - Either Portland cement mortar or a special cement-
lime mortar mixed in proportions of 1-part Portland cement, 1-part slaked lime
(lime putty) or dry hydrated lime, and not more than 4-parts of sand, shall be
used for walls of hollow unit construction.

       Working Stresses - The maximum allowable compressive stresses in masonry
of hollow tile, concrete block or concrete tile, due to combined live and dead
loads, shall not exceed eighty (80} pounds per square inch of gross sectional

of gross sectional area when laid with special cement~lime mortar.

       Sec. 77.   Heights, Hollow Unit Construction -

       (aJ Buildings under this section with bearing walls of hollow unit con-
struction, are limited. to one story in height when located within the Inner
Fire Limits al'ld shall not exceed three (3) stories when located outside the
Inner Fire Limits. The clear story heights shall not exceed fourteen (14} feet
for the first story, twelve (12) feet for the second and eleven (11} feet for
the third; PROVIDED, however, that in one story buildings with trusses overhead,
the story may be measured in the clear below main bottom chords of the trusses
when the said trusses rest on solid masonry piers built in the outside walls.

        (b} No eight (SJ inch hollow tile wall shall have a greater length than
Sixty ( 60) feet,. unless reinforced with a solid masonry pier, such piers shall
be not more than twenty (20} feet apart. Cross walls or substantial rigid par-
titions may be used in lieu of the piers. When such partitions are used, they
shall be securely bolted to the hollow tile walls, using at least three (3)
one-half (1) inch bolts in each story height; end studs adjacent to the tile wall
to be doubled. Cross walls and angles shall have full masonry bond with abutting
mr intersecting walls. When hollow tile walls are faced with brick or stone and
such facing is continuously bonded every eigriteen ( 18) inches in height with
hollow tile, using solid masonry bonding which laps onto hollow tile wall at
least four (4) inches, the facing may be figured as a part of the wall.

       (c) All lintels are to be reinforced for spans not exceeding five (5) feet
when the reinforcing rods are not less than one-half(½) inch square deformed
steel bars. Solid brick arches may be used when they start on solid masonry
skew backs. Heavy concentrated loads, ends of girders and trusses shall be
supported on appropriate solid masonry piers or approved equivalent.

       (d) Hollow tile walls and other masonry in conjunction therewith, shall be
carefully executed, using the same general precautions and workmanship as re-
quired for brick masonry except a s ~ be otherwise stipulated in this section.
All joists and rafters shall rest on solid rrasonry plate consisting of at least
two courses of brick, one header and one stretcher course, four (4J inches of
concrete, or solid tile plates at least one (1) inch thick; said joist and rafter
plates to be at least eight (8) inches wide, spaces between joists to be solidly
filled ,':i th brick or concrete masonry for full depths of joists where they rest
on the walls. :Beams, girders and trusses must have steel or iron bearing plE~tes
in addition to the solid masonry wall plates. Buildings exceeding two (2) stories
in height, shall have a reinforced concrete belt all around on outside walls and
placed under second floor joists, belt to be at least eight (8) inches by eight
(8) inches and to be reinforced with two ½-inch square continuous rods, lapped
eighteen (18) inches at splice.

      ': ( e) Hollow tile walls la.id up with the cells vertical, shall he,ve solid
masonry belt or bond course every fourth tile in height, said bond course shall
be the same thickness and materials as required for solid masonry bearing plates
in the preceding paragraph or a mesh bond may be used in lieu of the masonry bond,
provided that the mesh be galvanized No. 20 gauge, ¼-inch mesh hardware cloth or
metal lath full width of wall and well bedded in mortar. Carefully build in all
anchors, placing solid masonry around same. All hollow tile to be used for light
loading only.

            Sec. BO. Skeleton Constru.ction - Skeleton construction is a form of build-
     ing construction ,7herein all external and. internal load and stresses are transmitted
     to the foundation by a rigidly connected frame-work of metal or reinforced concrete.
     The enclosing walls are supported by spandrels at each story.

            The enclosing or panel walls, if of brick sla:all be not less than twelve (12)
     inches thick, laid in cement mot,tar. When the vertical distance between supporting
     girders exceeds fifteen (15) feet, the thickness of the wall shall be increased fov.r
     (4) inches for each fifteen (15) feet or fraction thereof that the said vertical
     distance exceeds fifteen (15) feet. Such walls shall be of brick, stone or gravel
     concrete, or hard burned terracotta.

            Terra Cotta blocks faced with brick bonded may be used for walls of skeleton
     construction.

             Walls mu.st be anchored at colu.inns, using metal anchors every twelve (12)
     inches in height or equi!~lent. Veneering at colurrms, beams and girders to be at least
     four l 4) inches and anch~s to structu.ra,l members not more than fourteen ( 14) inches
     apart measured in any direction. The same system of bonding to be used as required
     for regular hollow tile or brick walls. Wood and other combustible lintels are pro-
     hibited. l,1asonry mu.llions under two (2) feet in width mu.st be solid nasonry when less
     than twelve {12) inches thick. Cement-lime mortar or better to be used in every case.

        Sec. 93. General - To obtain a satisfactory structure, the following
major req_uirem.ents shall be fulfilled:.

        (al The material used shall be suitable, of uniform quality and with-•
out defects affecting the strength or service of the stru.cture.

                    (b) Proper loads and conditions shall be assumed in the design.

                    (c) The unit stresses shall be suitable for the material used.

        (d) The workmanship mu.st be good, so that defects or injuries are not
produced in the manufacture.

        ( e) The computations and design shall be properly made so that the unit
stresses specified shall not be exceeded, and the structure and its details
shall possess the requisite strength and rigidity.

         Sec. 94. Material - Structural steel shall conform to the Standard
Specifi<a"ations of the America:p. Society for Testing :Materials for Structural
Steel for Euildinr0s.
  I   •.   •   ••           ~J




                Sec. 95. Loading -

         (a} Steel structures shall be designed to sustain the dead weight im-
posed upon them, including the weight of the steel frame itself, and, in'"
addition 1 the maximum live load as specified in each particular case. Proper
provision shall be made for temporary stresses caused by erection.

        (b) In cases where live loads have the effect of producing impact or
vibration, a proper percentage shs,11 be added to the static 1i ve load stresses
to provide for such influences so that the total stress found in a;ny member is
an equivalent static stress.

           (c) Proper provision shall be rm.de for stresses caused by wind both
during erection and after completi0n of the building. The •vind pressure is
dependent upon the conditions of exposure, but the allowable stresses specified
in section 96, paragraphs (f} and (g),. are based upon the steel frame being
des:4:,oned to carry a wind pressI:i.re of not less than twenty (20} pounds per square
foot on the vertical projection of exposed surface during erection and fifteen
( 15) pounds per square foot on the verticai projection of the finished structure.

        {d} Proper provision shall 7Je made to securely fasten the reaction points
of all steel construction and transmit the stresses to the found.ations of the
structure. (See Sec. 143, Loadine Design)

        Sec. 111. Trimmers and Headers - Headers in floor framing in business
buildings carrying heavy loads placed more than two ( 2} feet from a trimmer,
shall be supported by iron stirrups. Every trimmer and header or tail joist
over four (4) feet long, in all buildings except dwelling houses and minor
structures and over eight (8) feet long in dwelling houses and minor stru.ctu.res,
shall be hung in stirrup irons or approved joist hangers of suitable strength
for the weight to be supported.

        Sec. 112. Double Joists - Wherever bearing partitions are located and
run parallel to joists, same"siw.11 be carried by joists well blocked with solid
bridging placed not more than sixteen (16) inches o.c. and securely nailed to-
gether or by double joists well spiked together.

        Sec. 113. Cutting of Beams -

        {a) Wood beams, girders, joists, rafters, columns or other supporting
timbers shall not be cut or bored when the cut is deeper or the center of the
bore is more than one-fifth (l/5) of the beam depth below the top, nor at any
point beyond three (3) times the depth of the beams from the end. Where cuts
in excess of the above and bore holes in excess of a diameter of two (2) inches
are necessary, special provisions, which shall be approved by the Chief Build-
ing Inspector for the framing of such beams, shall be made•
        (b} In basements the footings supporting wood columns shall extend not
less than two (2) inches above the finished floor. The ends of wood columns
in basements shall receive two brush applications of hot creosote.

        (c) All wood columns built in partitions in basements shall not be used
as foundations below the ground.
~
        sec. 114. Bridging -

         (a) All wooden floor beams or joists, except in mill construction, shall
be bridged with cross-bridging between supports if a span is over eight (8) feet.
The distance between bridging or between bridging and bearing shall not exceed
eight (8} feet. Greater spans shall have cross-bridging placed six (6) feet
center to center.

        (b) All wooden floor beams or joists shall be bri4ged with solid bridging
over bearing pe.rtitions when the underside of the joist is to be covered. Solid
bridging shall be not less than two (2) inches in thickness and shall be the full
height of the joistl Cross-bridging l1l8¥ be of 1-inch by 3-inch ( l"x3n) in size

        Sec. 124. Permit to Cover -

        (a) No person shall lath, plaster or otherwise cover or conceal any of
the spaces between studs, joists, framework or r-u.rring of any structure until he
has obtained a supplementary permit therefor.

        (b) No permit to lath, plaster or conceal any of the spaces between studs,
joists, framework or furring of any structure shall be issued until Chief Build-
ing Inspector has inspected and found that suitable fire· stops are placed, the
structure properly framed and braced, nor until the concealed work of the
electrical and plumbing systemw have been approved by nra.nicipal authority. The
Chief Building Inspector shall inspect same within forty-eight (48) hours after
receiving written notice. Sundays and legal holidays excepted.

        Sec. 125. Fire Stops -

        (a) All interior and exterior walls and floor or ceiling joist inter-
wections, shall be provided with suitable fire stopping in a manner to complete-
ly cut off communication by fire through concealed spaces and to prevent drafts
both vertical and horizontal.

        (b) All walls which are to be lathed and plastered or otherwise sheathed
on either side, all stud wa.lls and all pa,rtitions which rest directly over each
other, shall be completely fire-stopped with brickwork or other suitable incom-
bustible material at each floor level. The spaces between the ends of floor
joints which rest upon masonry walls, shall be filled properly with fire stopping
material to the full depth of the joist and spaces between the studs shall be
filled in the same manner to a height of six (6) inches above the floor level.
Fire stopping shall be arranged to cut off concealed draught openings and form
an effectual horizontal fire barrier between stories.

                                      -6-

=== page 54 ===
        (c) Wood fire blocks no less than two (2) inches in thickness and the full
width of the stud, shall be cut in between the studs just above the floor line
and just below the ceiling, but if there is a cove ceiling, the fire blocks shall
be lowered to the spring of the cove. A wood plate at least two (2) inches in
thickness and width of the studs, may be used in place of the cutting in a fire
block. Fire stops made of incombustible material such as mineral wood, concrete
or mortar, so as to prevent the passage of fire, may be used with the approval
of the Chief Building Inspector in place of wood fire stops.

          (d) In all stud partitions intermediate fire-stopping shall be placed be-
tween the studs for the full width of the studs midway between floor levels. Such
wooden fire stops in walls or partitions shall be wedged in between the studs at
an angle of not less than fifteen (15) degrees nor more than thirty (30) degrees
from the horizontal to avoid possible openings due to shrinke,ge and at the same
time stiffen the frame. No such horizontal bracing or fire stopping shall be less
than t1110 ( 2) inches in thickness.

         (e) When a partition rests directly on a wooden girder in a basenEnt or
where stud partitions rest directly over each other and run down between the
floor beams and rest upon the top of the partition below, a 25/32-inch board
should be nailed tp both outer faces of the studs for the entire length of the
partition between the joists and the spaces between the studs to four (4) inches
above the top of the floor be filled with incombustible material. If loose in-
combustible material is not used as a filling, a two (2) inch piece should be
wedged in between the studs horizontally, directly above the floor joist line.
Such stops shall be arranged to entirely separate the spaces between the floor
beams and those between the partition studs to effectually cut off draft.

         ( f) Where walls are studded off, the space between the inside of the wall
and the studding at the floor level, shall be fire stopped with brickwork or other
approved fireproof material. The beams dire_ctly over the studded off space shall
be deadened with not less than six (6) inches of fireproof material which shall
be laid on boards cut 1h between the beams. The underside of such bear.as shall be
protected by a covering of metal, metal la.th or plaster board and plastered to a
total thickness of three-~uarters (¾) of an inch:

        (g) Stair carriages shall be fire-stopped at least once in the middle
portion of each run and shall be fire-stopped by a header beam at the top and
bottom, so as to effectually prevent the passage of fire.

        (h) 'i:lhen sliding doors are pocketed in partitions, such pockets shall be
completely fire stopped at end, sides, top snd bottom.

        ( i) All spaces between chimneys and wooden beams shall be solidly filled
with refuse mortar, loose cinders, or other incombu:ztible material, or provided
with two (2) inch air space.

        Sec. 126. 1Jfasonr.;y: Supports - Mo masonry wall shall be superi:rrwosed upon
timber framing or U:<:)On structural steel unless steel is protected by a})proved
method of fireproofing.

        Sec. 127. Anchors - Floor joists above first floor, shall be anchored to
their bearing walls by steel or iron tee irons every fifth joist.

        Sec. 128. Fire Stoppii'lg ~rou,.~d Pipes - Where plumbing, heating, ventil-
ating and other pipes, conduits or speaking tubes pass through wooden floors or
wooden po,rti tions, they shall be boxed in e,nd fire stopped with incombustible
material so as to prevent spread of fire.

        Sec. 129. Clothes Chutes - All clothes chutes, dumb waiters, shafts, pipe
shafts and other enclosed spaces of similar chare,cter, extending from one story
to another, where not required by this ordin2,nce to be built of fire-resistive

openings shall be closed with tight-fitting metal lined covers or d.oors on both sides.

            Sec. 139. Floor Loads - Each floor of every building shall -be of suf-
     ficient strength in all its parts to bear safely the weight to be imposed there-
     on, in addition to the dead loads. The dead loads shall be taken as the actual
     weight of the floors, walls, roofs, partitions and all permanent construction;
     the live load, all other loads than dead loads. All partitions which are subject
     to removel or rearrangement shall be considered a.s live loads.

      Sec. 140. Stair and Stair Landing Loads - All stairs, platforms, landings,
balconies and stair hall~ays shall be of sufficient strength to sustain safe)Jr
a live load of not less than one hundred (lOOJ pounds per square foot for in-
terior construction and one hundred and fifty ( 150) pounds per square foot for
elitterior construction with a factor of safety of four (4) in each case.

     Sec. 141. Roof Loads - Every roof with a pitch of less than twenty f20} de-
grees with the horizontal shall be proportioned to bear safely a live load of
fifty (50) pounds per square.foot of surface. If the pitch of a roof is tw~nty
(20) degrees or more, the live load shall be assuned to be thirty {30) pounds per
square foot or roof surface considered as acting normal to the surface.

      Sec. 142. Colu1nn Loads -

      (a) Every column, post or other vertical support shall ~oe of sufficient
 strength to bear safely the combined live and dead loads transmitted to it.

      (b) In buildings more than five (5) stories in height, the following re-
ductions are permissible:

      I!'or columns supporting each succeeding floor, a reduction of five ( 5~0) per
 cent of the total load per floor may be made, but the total deduction shall not
 exceed fifty ( so;&} per cent.

     No reduction of live load on columns shall be permitted in buildings where
the assumed floor load is more than one hundred and twenty (120) pounds per
squane foot and is likely to be permanent in character, as in warehouses, print-
ing houses and machine shops.

     For structures carrying nachinery such as cranes, conveyors s,nd prinU,ng
presses, at least twenty-five {25'.}b} per cent shall be added to the stresses for
live loads to provide for effect of impact and vibrations.

1-    Sec. 143. Wind Pressure -

     (a) All buildings or parts of buildings in which the height is more than
three ( :,3 J times the minimum horizontal d_imention, shall be designed to resist
a horizontal wind pressure in any direction of twenty (20) pounds for every
square foot of exposed surface. '.Hnd bracing shall be provided by w2,king the
connect ion joists between girders e.nd co lurnns sufficient for the vertifal load
as well as the bending due to side pressure; or diagonal bracing shall be placed
between columns, proportioned to transfer the shear of the side pressure to the
footings. All details shall be designed to carry the stresses in the main members.,

(b) The overturning moment due to wind pressure shall not exceed fifty (50~&) of the
amount of stability of the structure, unless the structure is securely anchored to
the foundation. The anchors shall be of sufficient strength to safely carry the
excess overturning moment, without exceeding the allowable unit stresses tiven in
this code.

      ( c} '.;hen the stress due to the wind in aey member of connection amounts to

                                          -2-
ss

=== page 60 ===
less than fifty (50%) per cent of the total live and dead loads, it may be neg-
lected. When the stress due to the wind exceeds fifty (50%} per cent of the
stress due to the combined live and dead loads, all these stresses shall be added
together and the allows,ble unit stress for the total may be taken at fifty {so;t}
per cent in excess of the values allowed for various materials as defh1ed by
this code. In no case shall the section be less than required for the live and
dead load.

         Sec. 144. Safety of Design - All parts to every building shall be de-
signed to safely carry the loads to be imposed thereon and shall in all other
respects conform to good engineering practice. Where this code furnishes neither
a unit working stress nor a factor of safety for a material, the relations of
allowable working stress to ultimate strength, shall be determined by the Chief
:Building Inspector.

        Sec. 145. Tables - The following gro.up of tables has been prepared to
facilitate design by furnishi:n;:,; in convenieµt and comprl?hensive form, some ade-
quate means of determining .the lengths of joist and rafter spans for conditions
encountered in ordinary construction. •The fa.ct that the weights of flooring,
plaster, roof coverings and other items necessary in any computa,tions having to
do with joist and rafter lengths are not always at hand when computations of
this kind have to be rrade often leads to lack of uniformity, not only in the same
office, but on the same job. Such being the condition, it has been our purpose
to offer in readily accessible form, information with necessary supporting data
dealing with this general subject.       Knowing the kind of timber, the proper column
to refer to for span length can quickly be determined by examining the table of
allowable working stresses which appears below.· The computations to determine
the strength of the various members in these tables, are bs,sed on the actu...A.l and
not the nominal sizes of timbers.

                            lIEIGH'J!S




     (Yee. 147. Heights Allowable for Buildings_,_:)

       (a) Residence Districts - In a residence "An or a residence ":S" Dis-
trict, no a.'weiling shall be erected to a height in excess of thirty-five
{35} feet and no building shall be erected to a height in excess of fifty
(5~-} J~et. In a residence 11 cn districtt no building shall be erected to a
heig~~-in excess of fifty { 50) feet. In a residence "D" district or a
residence "En district, no build.ing she.11 be erected to a height in excess
of eighty (80} feet.

     i (b) Business Districts - In a business r'A 11 district, no garage build-
ing shall be erected to a height in excess of one (1) story; no store and
dwelling building shall be erected to a height in excess of two stories; 1:1,nd
no building shall be erected to a height in excess of fifty (50) feet. In a
business nB" district, no building shall be erected to a height in excess of
fifty ( 50:} feet. In a business ncu district, no build.ing shall be erected to   •f
a height in excess of eighty (80} feet.

       ( c) Commercial Districts - In a commercial 11 A11 district, no building
other tban a grain elevator or a gas holder shall be erected to a height in
excess of fifty (50) feet. In a commercial n:B 11 district, no building other
than a grain elevator or a gas holder shall be erected to a height in excess
of eighty (80} feet. In a commercial "en district; no building of non-fire-
resistive construction shall be erected to a height in excess of eighty (80)
feet and no building of fire-resistive construction shall be erected to a
height in excess of one hundred and fifty (150) feet.

      ( d} Industrial JJistricts - In an industrial uAn district or an indus-
trial n:r3n district, no building other than a grain elevator or a gas holder,
shall be erected to a height in excess of eighty (80) feet.

      (e} Exceptions -

      (1) The provisions of this section shall not apply to restrict the height
of a church spire, tower or belfry, or a flagpole, wi. re less tower, monument,
chimney, water tank, elevator bulkhead, sta.ge to,ver or scenery left. Nothing in
these regv.lations shall apply to prevent the erection above the height limit of
a parapet wall or cornice for ornament and without windows, extending above such
height limit not more than five (5} feet.

       ( 2} Any portion of a building may be erected to a height in excess of the
respective height limits as herein prescribed, provided such portion of such
building is set back from an street, alley and required ya.rd lines, one ( 1}
foot for each three ( 3) feet of such e,dditional height if in an industrial or
commercial district, and one (1) foot for each two (2) feet of such additional
height if in a business or residential district.

      (3) Height of buildings shall be taken as the vertical distance from the
curb level to the top of the highest point of the roof beams in the case of flat
roofs, or to the average height of the 5 ables in the case of roofs having a pitch
of more than twenty (20} degrees with a horizontal plane. When a. building faces
two or more streets having different grades, the measurement s.ball be taken at
the point of the average curb level. When. a building does not adjoin the street,

          Sec. 176. Chimneys for Low Pressure Boilers -

        (a} Chimneys of all low pressure boilers or furnaces, also the smoke flues
for bakers' ovens, large cooking ranges, large laundry stoves and all flues used
for similar purposes, shall be at least eight (SJ inches in thickness and be lined
continuously on the inside with well burned terracotta or fire clay chimney tile
set in Portland cement mortar. All such chimneys shall be capped with terra cotta,
stone, concrete or cast iron.

        (b) The smoke flue of every high pressure steam boiler and every appliance
producing a corresponding temperature in a flue, if built of brick, stone, rein-
forced concrete or other approved nasonry, shall have walls not less than twelve
(12) inches thick and the inside four (4) inches of such walls shall be fire brick,~
laid in Portland cement mortar for a distance of at least twenty-five (25) feet
from the point where the. smoke connection of the boiler enters the flue.

        Sec. 177. l'Ietal Smokestae.ks - 1\'Ietal smokestacks may be permitted for
boilers, fu.rne.ces and similar apparatus where large hot fires are used, provided
they have a clearance from all combustible naterial of not less than one-ha,lf (~)
of diameter of the stack, but not less than fifteen ( l.5} inches, unless the com-              "1
bustible material be properly gur.rded by loose fitting rretal shields, in which

          Sec. 179. Fireplaces -

        (a} The walls of fireplaces shall never be less than eight (8) inches thick
and if built of stone, the minimum shall be twelve ( 12) inches •

       . (b) When a grate is set in a fireplace, a lining of fire brick at least
two (2) inches in thiclo.1ess shall be added to this fireplace or soapstone; tile
or cast iron may be used if solidly backed with brick or concrete.

        (c) All fireplaces and chimney breasts shall have trinlli'ler arches or other
approved incombustible construction supporting hearths. The arches and hearths
shall be at least twenty (20) inches wide, measured from the face of the chimney
breast. The arches shall be of brick,stone or terracotta, not less than four
(4) inches thick. A flat stone or z:ei.nforced concrete slab may be used to carry
the hearth instead of the arch, if it is properly supported and a suitable fill
provided between it and the hearth. The length of the trimmer arches and hearth
shall not be less than twenty-four (24}~inches longer than the fireplace opening.
Hearths shall be of brick, stone, tile or concrete. Wood centering under a trim-
mer arch shali be removed before plasteriug the ceilings underneath.

          Sec. 196. Plans and Specifications - No new plant or reconstruction
  of any old plants for producing power and heat, or either of them, nor any
  new chimney connected therewith, except in apartment houses of twenty (20)
  rooms or less, shall be erected or reconstructed until plans and specifications
  of the same shall have been filed in the office of the Chief :euildin:e; Inspector
  and e.pproved by him and a JJerL1i t therefor issued by him for such erect ion or
  reconstruction. Such plans and specifications so to be filed shall show the
  amount of power to be developed, the amount of heating to be done by such plant
  and all appurtenances thereto, including all provisions rmde for the purpose
  of :proper combustion of the fuel to be used and for preventing smoke, together
  with the location within the building of such ple,nt and the provision to be
  made for suitable space for boilers, engine, plant arui fittings, and also for
  sui taole ventilation of the boiler and engine room or rooms. Upon the acpproval
  of such plans and specifications, or upon the approval of the plans and specifi-
  cations as they may be modified by the Chief Building Inspector (a duplicate of
  which plans shall be left on file in the office of said Chief Building Inspector},
  and upon the Pl\in:rent of the fees as hereinafter provided, the Chief :Building
  Inspector, after approval of said plans, shall issues, permit :for "the erection
  or reconstruction of any such plant; and it shall be the duty of the Chief
  Building Inspector to see that the execution of the 1:;ork so permitted is carried
  out in conformity with the plans and specifications so approved; and all work
  done under the permit issued as aforese. id shall conform to the plans s,nd specifi-
  cations so approved.

          Sec. 197. Density     CL1he emission of smoke of a density in excess of
  forty ( 40 J per cent, or equivalent, to Ifo. 2 of the Ring ler/1':1.:nn Scale of Smoke
  Densities, within the limits of the City and County of :Jenver, from any plant,
  steam locomotive or contrivance now existing or hereafter to be constructed, ex-
  cept for a period of fif·teen ( 15) minutes allowed for cleaning fire boxes or
  building new fires in any one hour, is hereby prohibited and any violation of

  shall be reported by said Chief J3uimdi:ng Inspector to the Smoke Cornrnis sion, snd
  such Commission may direct the prosecution of any offenders thereof.

         Sec. 198. Inspection - It is hereby made the duty of the Ghief :Building

       Seco 202. Exits -

       (a) Every building, except private dwellings, and each floor above the
first, shall be provided with at least two ( 2) means of egress remote from

to a street. The other may open to a yard or other si:iace deen~d safe by the
Chief Building Inspector. {See special req_uirements for the£,tre, dry cleaning
and garage buildings)

       {b} J31J_ildings of group I, II and III, and. over three (3) stories in
height, shall have not less than two {2) means of egress remote from each other,
one being an enclosed stairway of incomb1:rntible !!18,terial with approved firedoors
at each floor landing. Buildings of these groups altered or reconstructed after
the passage of this ordinance, rray use external standard fire escapes in lieu of
enclosed ste.irway if spgroved by the Chief :Building Imipector and the Chief of
the Fire :oepartment.

       {c) When exit doorways have a clear width of at least forty (40) inches
each, the aggregste width of doorways sru.~11 be eqUEcl to the re~uired width of
corridor or stairway served by sane. 1:IT1en individual doors are less than forty
(40) inches wide, there shall be one doorway for each twenty-two (22) inches of
required width of corridor leading to same. Every doorway shall be at least
twenty-eight (28) inches wide in the clear. All passageways and exit doors shall
swing in the direction of exit travel.

       Sec. 200. Stairs, Halls and Entrances-

                  (c) Enforcing J:'u.blic Safety - 1.I1he enforcing of provisions for public safety in

                Sec. 251. Definitions - For the pUJ:1)ose of this ordinance, the
    term ttpublic Garagen is defined as being a building, lot, parcel of land, or

             Sec. 316. ll'ences - No fence within the City and County of Denver, con-
      structed back of the front or side building line of any building facing on

      be constructed of open picket or similar fence work; Provided, that in case no
      building is constructed upon a lot, then no fence thereon shall exceed three (3}
      feet in height.
